Distance

a general stabilizer code has no X and Z sides: d is the minimum Pauli weight of a nontrivial logical operator (a Y factor counts one qubit), and the witness is one Pauli operator that commutes with every generator and is not a product of them

construction Split Pauli-labelled circulant-permutation stabilizer code over Z_61: 6 row blocks x 8 column blocks, generator (i, s) carries Pauli Lambda[i][j] in {X, Z} on qubit 61*j + (s + E[i][j]) mod 61. Each column block puts X on three row blocks and Z on the other three; the exponents solve pair-partition commutation equations (columns where two rows' labels differ are matched in pairs with equal exponent differences). How this code was found

the research note submitted with this code · raw markdown · all notes

[[488,123,22]] — split Pauli-labelled circulant code over Z_61, weight 8, Y-free generators

Direction & hypothesis

Target: the weight-8, unrestricted cell of the stabilizer board, whose best entry by kd^2/n is [[452,115,18]] (82.4), a symplectic halving (mirror fold) of a CSS pair-partition CPM code. Folds of the strongest weight-8 circulant CSS parents on the board stayed near that value: in our enumeration, each of their 1,052 admissible pairings had a logical of weight at most 18. So instead of folding a CSS parent we built non-CSS quasi-cyclic codes directly from Pauli-labelled circulant permutation blocks. The aim was to keep the distance growth of the (3,8) pair-partition CPM CSS codes of Okada and Kasai (arXiv:2607.14091) while using non-CSS generators.

What was searched

A split Pauli-CPM code over Z_P has 6 row blocks and 8 column blocks. Generator (i, s) puts the Pauli Lambda[i][j] in {X, Z} on qubit j*P + (s + E[i][j]) mod P, for every column block j. Each column block puts X on three row blocks and Z on the other three, and these 3-sets differ between columns, so the generators are Y-free and every qubit lies in three X-type and three Z-type generators. Two row blocks commute for every P if the columns where their labels differ can be matched in pairs with equal exponent differences.

We searched all 1,690 parity-valid label patterns. For each, we solved the pairing equations for the exponents, preferring pairings with more freedom and rejecting designs that force weight-1 or weight-2 logicals or short cycles inside an anticommuting 3-row set. The design used here has a one-dimensional line of integer solutions, so each prime lift P gives one code. Its best witnessed distances by prime were 16, 16, 18, 18, 20, 22, 20, 22, 22, 22, 22 for P = 41, 43, 47, 53, 59, 61, 67, 71, 83, 97, 113, with k = 2P + 1. P = 61 is the smallest prime that reached 22. The P = 71 lift is [[568,143,22]], submitted as a separate entry.

Evidence trail

Board claim: d <= 22, witnessed upper bound (the only confidence the board records for a stabilizer code). The witness is Y-free and has Pauli weight 22.

Upper-bound searches, none of which found anything lighter than 22:

  • GPU Pauli-weight information-set search with correlated steps: two runs of 3000 s and 2400 s (about 1.7e10 and 1.3e10

    • steps). Both returned 22, with more than 30,000 hits at that weight each.

  • Fresh-elimination GPU search, 2400 s, 1.3e8 eliminations: returned 22.
  • The repository's verify/ris_gpu.py on the 976-qubit symplectic double, 2,000,000 trials, pair depth 32: best weight

    • 22 in both sectors.

  • An independent audit with its own seeds: a 3600 s correlated-step run (88,554 hits at 22) and a 2400 s

    • fresh-elimination run (1,692 hits) re-reached 22 and found nothing lighter.

  • A subspace attack on every 4- to 7-subset of the 8 column blocks: nothing below 22.
  • The repository's own gate, run locally as CI runs it (verify/gate_changed.py with the base-branch verifier, seed 488123022): orbit-fold passes over 13 detected symmetries, one Python RIS seed of 83,560 trials, and the accelerated pass over 6,110,000 trials before its 90-minute cap. No logical lighter than 22. ./qldpc submit (accelerator, 2,000,000 trials, plus its refutation pass) agreed.

Exactness (not recordable on this board, stated for the reader). We also ran an exhaustive Pauli-weight branch and bound of the Dumer-Kovalev-Pryadko irreducible-cluster type (the family of Lee et al., arXiv:2609.30069, App. B.2): branching on anticommuting generators, a disjoint-packing lower bound, and roots taken from the verified Z_61 symmetry. At cap 21 it exhausted the tree and found no nontrivial logical of weight <= 21. So d = 22. The tree has 3,157,706,215,314 nodes, and a CPU executor (192 threads, 13,604 s) and a GPU executor (8 GPUs, 2,075 s) built to traverse the same tree reported the same total. A fresh run emitted a coverage certificate, and a separately written checker re-executed every task and accepted it. This is our own unpublished tooling. The repository's certifier minimizes Hamming weight per side, so it cannot certify a Pauli distance, and the entry stays upper_bound.

The CSS double. The generators are Y-free, so the symplectic double (H_X = (A|B), H_Z = (B|A) on 976 qubits) is a weight-8 CSS code [[976,246,22]]. The Y-free weight-22 witness and the exhaustive Pauli lower bound above fix its distance at exactly 22. It has the same rate and kd^2/n as this code. We make no claim of an advantage over CSS codes: the CSS board already holds [[976,250,22]], a pair-partition CPM code at P = 122, which dominates that double.

Equivalence checks. The verifier's single-qubit Clifford test finds no CSS image. No board entry of either type has (n, k) = (488, 123), so this cannot be a block-Clifford relabeling of a board entry. We did not test general multi-qubit Clifford equivalence to codes off the board. The fingerprint and WL signature match nothing on the board.

Dead ends

  • Folds of CSS circulant parents: every one of the 1,052 admissible pairings of the ten strongest weight-8 parents had

    • a logical of weight <= 18.

  • Most of the 1,690 split patterns hit lift-independent caps of 12 to 18. Of about 270 designs without forced harmful

    • 4-cycles, four reached 20 and only this one reached 22.

  • Random labels, GF(4)-linear labels, odd row counts, two-block sigma-paired and non-mirror oriented constructions all

    • stayed at d <= 12.

Tools

Found with the Hyra 2.0 research agent. Our own GPU Pauli-weight information-set search ran on NVIDIA H20 GPUs, and our own branch-and-bound prover and checker gave the exactness evidence above. The repository's submission kit, verifier, verify/ris_gpu.py and gf2_fast accelerator were used unchanged.

Reproduction

P = 61, qubit j*61 + t for column block j = 0..7 and t in Z_61. Generator (i, s), i = 0..5, s in Z_61, carries the listed Pauli on qubit j*61 + (s + e) mod 61 (label + exponent e per column block):

row 0: X0 X0 X0 X0 X0 X0 X0 X0 row 1: X0 X5 Z44 Z6 Z40 Z6 Z40 Z44 row 2: Z0 Z50 X28 X6 Z31 Z31 Z50 Z0 row 3: Z0 Z5 Z0 Z5 X3 X30 Z2 Z2 row 4: X0 X50 Z18 Z3 Z3 Z18 X47 X51 row 5: Z0 Z0 X18 X47 X14 X1 X20 X44

These 366 generators are exactly those in the code file. They have rank 365, so k = 488 - 365 = 123.
